Well, hello, Doctor. Thank you so much for fitting me in. I, uh, I hope I'm not wasting your time with this... it's probably not a huge deal, but I wanted to make sure I'm doing the right thing by coming in. 

I guess I came in because... well, I've been sort of steering clear of my own workshop lately. And, um, I've also been skipping out on any family get-togethers if my brother-in-law might be there. I know it probably sounds a bit foolish for a man my age to avoid things like that—I hope you don't think I'm just being difficult or stubborn. It's just that, after all this time, I think I'm getting really tired of planning my whole life around not running into those reminders. 

Does that make sense, Doctor? I hope I'm explaining it okay.

I don't really know what specific thing it is about the workshop, or... or him, for that matter. I'm sorry, I know that's probably not very helpful for your notes. I hope I'm not making things too difficult for you by not having a better explanation for it. I just really don't know. Is it alright if I'm not entirely sure?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Oh, well, I still manage the basics just fine, Doctor. I really try to be responsible and not let things fall by the wayside. I mean... I suppose, if I'm being completely honest, I have stepped back from a few things. 

Like... well, my regular volunteer jobs. They involved the workshop, you see, so it just seemed easier to politely bow out. And I haven't been to some of the big family events lately, either. I just... I didn't want to cause any awkwardness for the rest of the family if he was there. I hope you don't think I'm being a terrible family man by doing that. I really just wanted to keep the peace, you know? 

And, um, my painting comes in fits and starts now. I kept most of my good supplies out in that place, so since I've been steering clear of it... I just haven't been able to paint like I used to. It's terribly silly of me to let it get in the way of my hobbies, isn't it? I really am keeping up with everything else, I promise. Does that sound like I'm handling things alright?

I haven't really noticed a specific pattern to it, to be perfectly honest. \n\nI hope it's alright that I don't have a better answer for you. I know you're asking good questions and trying to help me get to the bottom of this, and I feel terrible being so vague. I just haven't been able to sort it out in my own head. I hope I'm not making your job too difficult today... is it okay if I'm just not sure?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel afraid when you think about going to the workshop or seeing your brother-in-law?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say I feel afraid, Doctor. I really don't think it's fear. I mean, I'm a grown man, it would be awfully silly of me to be scared of my own workshop, wouldn't it? \n\nIt's more just... well, I guess I just don't really know how to describe the feeling. It's just an unpleasant sort of unease, maybe? But definitely not fear. I hope that's an okay answer... I really don't want you to think I'm some sort of coward, avoiding things out of fright. That's not it at all, I promise. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this unpleasant unease about going to the workshop or seeing your brother-in-law?\n\nPatient: Well, let's see... I suppose it's been a little while now. It all started right around the time I had that, well, that disagreement with my brother-in-law. That was about two years and four months ago, give or take. \n\nI really haven't meant to let it drag on this long, Doctor. I know I probably should have just gotten over it and gone back into the workshop by now. I hope you don't think I'm just being stubborn or holding a grudge all this time. I really try to be a forgiving person, I do. It's just that the unease kind of stuck around longer than I expected. Is it... is it normal for something like that to bother a person for a couple of years? I hope I'm not making too big a deal out of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeatedly thinking about the disagreement with your brother-in-law when you're not trying to?\n\nPatient: Well, I hate to admit it, Doctor, because I really do try to just let bygones be bygones... but, yes, I suppose it does come up when I'm not trying to think about it. I'll just be going about my day, and out of nowhere, I'll see his hand with that heavy tool again. It's like a... a picture that just pops into my head while I'm doing something completely normal, like shaving in the morning, or working on one of my paintings. \n\nIt's awfully silly of me to let it interrupt my day like that, isn't it? It still makes my chest tighten up a bit when it happens, even though I try to just shake it off right away and get back to what I was doing. I hope you don't think I'm sitting around brooding over it on purpose. It really just happens on its own... is that the sort of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Does this unease affect your daily activities or relationships with others outside of avoiding the workshop and your brother-in-law?\n\nPatient: Oh, well, I still manage the basics just fine, Doctor. I really try to be responsible and not let things fall by the wayside. I mean... I suppose, if I'm being completely honest, I have stepped back from a few things. \n\nLike... well, my regular volunteer jobs. They involved the workshop, you see, so it just seemed easier to politely bow out. And I haven't been to some of the big family events lately, either. I just... I didn't want to cause any awkwardness for the rest of the family if he was there. I hope you don't think I'm being a terrible family man by doing that. I really just wanted to keep the peace, you know? \n\nAnd, um, my painting comes in fits and starts now. I kept most of my good supplies out in that place, so since I've been steering clear of it... I just haven't been able to paint like I used to. It's terribly silly of me to let it get in the way of my hobbies, isn't it? I really am keeping up with everything else, I promise. Does that sound like I'm handling things alright?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have memories or pictures in your mind of the disagreement with your brother-in-law that feel very real or vivid?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, Doctor, nothing quite like that. I don't feel like I'm actually right back there in the room with him or anything frightening like that. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well... oh, I don't know if "guilty" is the right word. It's just... sometimes I get to thinking, and I can't help but feel like I... I should have handled things differently. 

I don't want to sound like I'm throwing a pity party, but I keep telling myself I should have just put my foot down sooner, you know? I never should have let him stay with us in the first place. I just... I think maybe none of this would have happened if I'd just handled it like a man right from the start. 

I know it's probably silly to dwell on it, and I really try not to let it get me down too much. I hope you don't think I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself. It's just a thought that creeps in sometimes. Is it normal to feel a little responsible like that? I'm trying to look at it the right way, I promise.

Well, I... I suppose there are a few little things, but nothing too terrible, I promise. I mean, I still manage all the basics just fine—getting dressed, making meals, all that. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around doing nothing.

I, uh, I hope I'm not wasting your time with this... it's probably not a huge deal, but I wanted to make sure I'm doing the right thing by coming in. \n\nI guess I came in because... well, I've been sort of steering clear of my own workshop lately. And, um, I've also been skipping out on any family get-togethers if my brother-in-law might be there. I know it probably sounds a bit foolish for a man my age to avoid things like that—I hope you don't think I'm just being difficult or stubborn. It's just that, after all this time, I think I'm getting really tired of planning my whole life around not running into those reminders. \n\nDoes that make sense, Doctor? I hope I'm explaining it okay.\n\nDoctor: What are these reminders that you're trying to avoid?\n\nPatient: Well, Doctor, I... I wish I could give you a good answer for that. To be perfectly honest, I'm not really sure what exactly they are. \n\nI just haven't been able to pin it down. It's more of just a... a feeling I get, I suppose. I don't really know what specific thing it is about the workshop, or... or him, for that matter. I'm sorry, I know that's probably not very helpful for your notes. I hope I'm not making things too difficult for you by not having a better explanation for it. I just really don't know. Is it alright if I'm not entirely sure?\n\nDoctor: Is it the thought of being in the workshop or seeing your brother-in-law that triggers this feeling, or is it something else entirely?\n\nPatient: Well, gee, Doctor... I really wish I could tell you. I've tried to think on it, I really have, but I just don't know if it's the thought of those things, or... or if it's something else entirely. I haven't really noticed a specific pattern to it, to be perfectly honest. \n\nI hope it's alright that I don't have a better answer for you. I know you're asking good questions and trying to help me get to the bottom of this, and I feel terrible being so vague. I just haven't been able to sort it out in my own head. I hope I'm not making your job too difficult today... is it okay if I'm just not sure?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel afraid when you think about going to the workshop or seeing your brother-in-law?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say I feel afraid, Doctor. I really don't think it's fear. I mean, I'm a grown man, it would be awfully silly of me to be scared of my own workshop, wouldn't it? \n\nIt's more just... well, I guess I just don't really know how to describe the feeling. It's just an unpleasant sort of unease, maybe? But definitely not fear. I hope that's an okay answer... I really don't want you to think I'm some sort of coward, avoiding things out of fright. That's not it at all, I promise. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this unpleasant unease about going to the workshop or seeing your brother-in-law?\n\nPatient: Well, let's see... I suppose it's been a little while now. It all started right around the time I had that, well, that disagreement with my brother-in-law. That was about two years and four months ago, give or take. \n\nI really haven't meant to let it drag on this long, Doctor. I know I probably should have just gotten over it and gone back into the workshop by now. I hope you don't think I'm just being stubborn or holding a grudge all this time. I really try to be a forgiving person, I do. It's just that the unease kind of stuck around longer than I expected. Is it... is it normal for something like that to bother a person for a couple of years? I hope I'm not making too big a deal out of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeatedly thinking about the disagreement with your brother-in-law when you're not trying to?\n\nPatient: Well, I hate to admit it, Doctor, because I really do try to just let bygones be bygones... but, yes, I suppose it does come up when I'm not trying to think about it. I'll just be going about my day, and out of nowhere, I'll see his hand with that heavy tool again. It's like a... a picture that just pops into my head while I'm doing something completely normal, like shaving in the morning, or working on one of my paintings. \n\nIt's awfully silly of me to let it interrupt my day like that, isn't it? It still makes my chest tighten up a bit when it happens, even though I try to just shake it off right away and get back to what I was doing. I hope you don't think I'm sitting around brooding over it on purpose. It really just happens on its own... is that the sort of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Does this unease affect your daily activities or relationships with others outside of avoiding the workshop and your brother-in-law?\n\nPatient: Oh, well, I still manage the basics just fine, Doctor. I really try to be responsible and not let things fall by the wayside. I mean... I suppose, if I'm being completely honest, I have stepped back from a few things. \n\nLike... well, my regular volunteer jobs. They involved the workshop, you see, so it just seemed easier to politely bow out. And I haven't been to some of the big family events lately, either. I just... I didn't want to cause any awkwardness for the rest of the family if he was there. I hope you don't think I'm being a terrible family man by doing that. I really just wanted to keep the peace, you know? \n\nAnd, um, my painting comes in fits and starts now. I kept most of my good supplies out in that place, so since I've been steering clear of it... I just haven't been able to paint like I used to. It's terribly silly of me to let it get in the way of my hobbies, isn't it? I really am keeping up with everything else, I promise. Does that sound like I'm handling things alright?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have memories or pictures in your mind of the disagreement with your brother-in-law that feel very real or vivid?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, Doctor, nothing quite like that. I mean, like I was saying before about that little picture popping into my head... it's really just a thought. I don't feel like I'm actually right back there in the room with him or anything frightening like that. I always know I'm just standing at my bathroom sink, or sitting in my living room. \n\nI really hope I didn't make it sound worse than it actually is when we were talking earlier. I wouldn't want you to think I'm losing my grip on reality or making a mountain out of a molehill! It's really just a bothersome memory that passes by, and I know right where I am the whole time. I hope I'm explaining that alright? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: Are there other things or situations that you avoid besides the workshop and your brother-in-law?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, Doctor, just those two things, really. I try very hard not to let this whole silly business dictate my life, you know? It's just the workshop and... well, him. \n\nI really do make an effort to go everywhere else I normally would. I still go to the grocery store, and the pharmacy, and I take my walks around the neighborhood just like always. I wouldn't want you to think I'm hiding away in my house or anything foolish like that! I'm keeping up with my usual routine as best as I can, aside from that one little area. I hope that shows I'm trying to be sensible about all this... does that sound like I'm doing the right thing?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like this unease has significantly impacted your overall quality of life or relationships with people other than your brother-in-law?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no, I don't think so. I really try my best not to let it spill over into everything else. My relationships with the rest of the family and my friends are just fine, really. Like I mentioned before, I might skip a big gathering if I know he's going to be there, just to keep the peace, but I still call my kids every week and chat with the neighbors over the fence. I make absolutely sure of it. \n\nI really wouldn't say my whole quality of life is ruined or anything dramatic like that. I'm still enjoying a quiet retirement, for the most part. I just... well, I try to focus on the good things so I don't become a burden or a downer to anyone else. I hope that's the right way to look at it? I really am trying to handle it all as best as I can. Does it seem like I'm managing okay to you?\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid the workshop or your brother-in-law because you're trying to prevent yourself from feeling anxious or uneasy?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, Doctor, I suppose that's exactly it. I don't like to admit it, because it feels a bit silly for a man my age to act this way, but I really just want to keep from feeling that... that bother. \n\nThat's actually the main reason I finally decided to come in and talk to you. I just keep steering clear of my own workshop, and, well, any family get-together where my brother-in-law might show up. I've been telling myself I'm just keeping the peace, but after all this time, I'm honestly just tired of planning my whole life around not running into those reminders. \n\nI hope you don't think less of me for letting it get to me like this. I really don't want to sound weak or foolish. I just want to handle things the right way, and I figured it was time to ask for a little advice. Do you think it makes sense that I'd get tired of avoiding it all? I hope I did the right thing by coming in.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that happened in the past?\n\nPatient: Well... oh, I don't know if \"guilty\" is the right word. It's just... sometimes I get to thinking, and I can't help but feel like I... I should have handled things differently. \n\nI don't want to sound like I'm throwing a pity party, but I keep telling myself I should have just put my foot down sooner, you know? I never should have let him stay with us in the first place. I just... I think maybe none of this would have happened if I'd just handled it like a man right from the start. \n\nI know it's probably silly to dwell on it, and I really try not to let it get me down too much. I hope you don't think I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself. It's just a thought that creeps in sometimes. Is it normal to feel a little responsible like that? I'm trying to look at it the right way, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious most of the time, even when you're not thinking about the workshop or your brother-in-law?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no, Doctor, I wouldn't say that at all. Most of the time, I'm right as rain. I try to stay busy, you know, puttering around the yard or reading the paper. I really do try to keep a positive attitude about things. \n\nIt's really only when those specific things come up that I feel that... that tightness. Otherwise, I'm just living a quiet life, same as anyone else my age. I'm not walking around wringing my hands all day, I promise you that. I don't want you to think I'm completely falling apart or anything. Does that sound about right to you? I hope I'm explaining it clearly enough.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your overall daily functioning or relationships outside of avoiding the workshop and your brother-in-law?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I suppose there are a few little things, but nothing too terrible, I promise. I mean, I still manage all the basics just fine—getting dressed, making meals, all that. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around doing nothing. \n\nBut, well... I used to do some regular volunteer work, you know, fixing things up for the church bazaar and things like that. I've sort of... stepped back from that, just because it usually meant I needed to use the workshop, or it involved big family events where... well, where he might be. And my painting—I used to paint a fair bit—it's been coming in fits and starts lately. Most of my good supplies are out in the shop, and I just haven't felt up to going out there to get them. \n\nI know it probably sounds like I'm letting this beat me, and I really feel foolish even admitting it. I'm trying my best to keep up with things, I really am. Do you think it's awful that I've let it affect my hobbies like that? I hope you understand I'm not trying to be lazy or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still avoid the workshop and your brother-in-law if you didn't feel uneasy about it?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, Doctor, I don't think so. If I didn't get that... that uneasy feeling, I'd be out there tinkering every day, just like I used to. \n\nHonestly, that's really the main reason I finally decided to come in and talk to you. I just keep steering clear of my own workshop, and... well, any family get-together where my brother-in-law might show up. After all this time, I'm just getting so tired of planning my whole life around making sure I don't run into those reminders. \n\nIt sounds a bit silly when I say it out loud, doesn't it? A grown man avoiding his own backyard and his family. I really want to get back to normal so I don't have to be such a bother to everyone with all my excuses. Does that make sense to you? I hope I'm doing the right thing by trying to finally tackle this.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I really wish I could tell you. I've tried to think on it, I really have, but I just don't know if it's the thought of those things, or... or if it's something else entirely. I haven't really noticed a specific pattern to it, to be perfectly honest. \n\nI hope it's alright that I don't have a better answer for you. I know you're asking good questions and trying to help me get to the bottom of this, and I feel terrible being so vague. I just haven't been able to sort it out in my own head. I hope I'm not making your job too difficult today... is it okay if I'm just not sure?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel afraid when you think about going to the workshop or seeing your brother-in-law?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say I feel afraid, Doctor. I really don't think it's fear. I mean, I'm a grown man, it would be awfully silly of me to be scared of my own workshop, wouldn't it? \n\nIt's more just... well, I guess I just don't really know how to describe the feeling. It's just an unpleasant sort of unease, maybe? But definitely not fear. I hope that's an okay answer... I really don't want you to think I'm some sort of coward, avoiding things out of fright. That's not it at all, I promise. That was about two years and four months ago, give or take. \n\nI really haven't meant to let it drag on this long, Doctor. I know I probably should have just gotten over it and gone back into the workshop by now. I hope you don't think I'm just being stubborn or holding a grudge all this time. I'll just be going about my day, and out of nowhere, I'll see his hand with that heavy tool again. I didn't want to cause any awkwardness for the rest of the family if he was there. I hope you don't think I'm being a terrible family man by doing that. I really just wanted to keep the peace, you know? \n\nAnd, um, my painting comes in fits and starts now. I kept most of my good supplies out in that place, so since I've been steering clear of it... I just haven't been able to paint like I used to. It's terribly silly of me to let it get in the way of my hobbies, isn't it? I really am keeping up with everything else, I promise. Does that sound like I'm handling things alright?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have memories or pictures in your mind of the disagreement with your brother-in-law that feel very real or vivid?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, Doctor, nothing quite like that. I mean, like I was saying before about that little picture popping into my head... it's really just a thought. I don't feel like I'm actually right back there in the room with him or anything frightening like that. I haven't really noticed a specific pattern to it, to be perfectly honest. \n\nI hope it's alright that I don't have a better answer for you. I know you're asking good questions and trying to help me get to the bottom of this, and I feel terrible being so vague. I just haven't been able to sort it out in my own head. I hope I'm not making your job too difficult today... is it okay if I'm just not sure?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel afraid when you think about going to the workshop or seeing your brother-in-law?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say I feel afraid, Doctor. I really don't think it's fear. I mean, I'm a grown man, it would be awfully silly of me to be scared of my own workshop, wouldn't it? \n\nIt's more just... well, I guess I just don't really know how to describe the feeling. It's just an unpleasant sort of unease, maybe? But definitely not fear. I hope that's an okay answer... I really don't want you to think I'm some sort of coward, avoiding things out of fright. That's not it at all, I promise. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this unpleasant unease about going to the workshop or seeing your brother-in-law?\n\nPatient: Well, let's see... I suppose it's been a little while now. It all started right around the time I had that, well, that disagreement with my brother-in-law. That was about two years and four months ago, give or take. \n\nI really haven't meant to let it drag on this long, Doctor. I know I probably should have just gotten over it and gone back into the workshop by now. I hope you don't think I'm just being stubborn or holding a grudge all this time. I really try to be a forgiving person, I do. It's just that the unease kind of stuck around longer than I expected. Is it... is it normal for something like that to bother a person for a couple of years? I hope I'm not making too big a deal out of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeatedly thinking about the disagreement with your brother-in-law when you're not trying to?\n\nPatient: Well, I hate to admit it, Doctor, because I really do try to just let bygones be bygones... but, yes, I suppose it does come up when I'm not trying to think about it. I'll just be going about my day, and out of nowhere, I'll see his hand with that heavy tool again. It's like a... a picture that just pops into my head while I'm doing something completely normal, like shaving in the morning, or working on one of my paintings. \n\nIt's awfully silly of me to let it interrupt my day like that, isn't it? It still makes my chest tighten up a bit when it happens, even though I try to just shake it off right away and get back to what I was doing. I hope you don't think I'm sitting around brooding over it on purpose. It really just happens on its own... is that the sort of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Does this unease affect your daily activities or relationships with others outside of avoiding the workshop and your brother-in-law?\n\nPatient: Oh, well, I still manage the basics just fine, Doctor. I really try to be responsible and not let things fall by the wayside. I mean... I suppose, if I'm being completely honest, I have stepped back from a few things. \n\nLike... well, my regular volunteer jobs. They involved the workshop, you see, so it just seemed easier to politely bow out. And I haven't been to some of the big family events lately, either. I just... I didn't want to cause any awkwardness for the rest of the family if he was there. I hope you don't think I'm being a terrible family man by doing that. I really just wanted to keep the peace, you know? \n\nAnd, um, my painting comes in fits and starts now. I kept most of my good supplies out in that place, so since I've been steering clear of it... I just haven't been able to paint like I used to. It's terribly silly of me to let it get in the way of my hobbies, isn't it? I really am keeping up with everything else, I promise. Does that sound like I'm handling things alright?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have memories or pictures in your mind of the disagreement with your brother-in-law that feel very real or vivid?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, Doctor, nothing quite like that. I mean, like I was saying before about that little picture popping into my head... it's really just a thought. I don't feel like I'm actually right back there in the room with him or anything frightening like that. I always know I'm just standing at my bathroom sink, or sitting in my living room. \n\nI really hope I didn't make it sound worse than it actually is when we were talking earlier. I wouldn't want you to think I'm losing my grip on reality or making a mountain out of a molehill! It's really just a bothersome memory that passes by, and I know right where I am the whole time. I hope I'm explaining that alright? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: Are there other things or situations that you avoid besides the workshop and your brother-in-law?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, Doctor, just those two things, really. I try very hard not to let this whole silly business dictate my life, you know? It's just the workshop and... well, him. \n\nI really do make an effort to go everywhere else I normally would. I still go to the grocery store, and the pharmacy, and I take my walks around the neighborhood just like always. I wouldn't want you to think I'm hiding away in my house or anything foolish like that! I'm keeping up with my usual routine as best as I can, aside from that one little area. I hope that shows I'm trying to be sensible about all this... does that sound like I'm doing the right thing?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like this unease has significantly impacted your overall quality of life or relationships with people other than your brother-in-law?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no, I don't think so. I really try my best not to let it spill over into everything else. My relationships with the rest of the family and my friends are just fine, really. Like I mentioned before, I might skip a big gathering if I know he's going to be there, just to keep the peace, but I still call my kids every week and chat with the neighbors over the fence. I make absolutely sure of it. \n\nI really wouldn't say my whole quality of life is ruined or anything dramatic like that. I'm still enjoying a quiet retirement, for the most part. I just... well, I try to focus on the good things so I don't become a burden or a downer to anyone else. I hope that's the right way to look at it? I really am trying to handle it all as best as I can. Does it seem like I'm managing okay to you?\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid the workshop or your brother-in-law because you're trying to prevent yourself from feeling anxious or uneasy?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, Doctor, I suppose that's exactly it. I don't like to admit it, because it feels a bit silly for a man my age to act this way, but I really just want to keep from feeling that... that bother. \n\nThat's actually the main reason I finally decided to come in and talk to you. I just keep steering clear of my own workshop, and, well, any family get-together where my brother-in-law might show up. I've been telling myself I'm just keeping the peace, but after all this time, I'm honestly just tired of planning my whole life around not running into those reminders. \n\nI hope you don't think less of me for letting it get to me like this. I really don't want to sound weak or foolish. I just want to handle things the right way, and I figured it was time to ask for a little advice. Do you think it makes sense that I'd get tired of avoiding it all? I hope I did the right thing by coming in.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that happened in the past?\n\nPatient: Well... oh, I don't know if \"guilty\" is the right word. It's just... sometimes I get to thinking, and I can't help but feel like I... I should have handled things differently. \n\nI don't want to sound like I'm throwing a pity party, but I keep telling myself I should have just put my foot down sooner, you know? I never should have let him stay with us in the first place. I just... I think maybe none of this would have happened if I'd just handled it like a man right from the start. \n\nI know it's probably silly to dwell on it, and I really try not to let it get me down too much. I hope you don't think I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself. It's just a thought that creeps in sometimes. Is it normal to feel a little responsible like that? I'm trying to look at it the right way, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious most of the time, even when you're not thinking about the workshop or your brother-in-law?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no, Doctor, I wouldn't say that at all. Most of the time, I'm right as rain. I try to stay busy, you know, puttering around the yard or reading the paper. I really do try to keep a positive attitude about things. \n\nIt's really only when those specific things come up that I feel that... that tightness. Otherwise, I'm just living a quiet life, same as anyone else my age. I'm not walking around wringing my hands all day, I promise you that. I don't want you to think I'm completely falling apart or anything. Does that sound about right to you? I hope I'm explaining it clearly enough.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your overall daily functioning or relationships outside of avoiding the workshop and your brother-in-law?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I suppose there are a few little things, but nothing too terrible, I promise. I mean, I still manage all the basics just fine—getting dressed, making meals, all that. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around doing nothing. \n\nBut, well... I used to do some regular volunteer work, you know, fixing things up for the church bazaar and things like that. I've sort of... stepped back from that, just because it usually meant I needed to use the workshop, or it involved big family events where... well, where he might be. And my painting—I used to paint a fair bit—it's been coming in fits and starts lately. Most of my good supplies are out in the shop, and I just haven't felt up to going out there to get them. \n\nI know it probably sounds like I'm letting this beat me, and I really feel foolish even admitting it. I'm trying my best to keep up with things, I really am. Do you think it's awful that I've let it affect my hobbies like that? I hope you understand I'm not trying to be lazy or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still avoid the workshop and your brother-in-law if you didn't feel uneasy about it?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, Doctor, I don't think so. If I didn't get that... that uneasy feeling, I'd be out there tinkering every day, just like I used to. \n\nHonestly, that's really the main reason I finally decided to come in and talk to you. I just keep steering clear of my own workshop, and... well, any family get-together where my brother-in-law might show up. After all this time, I'm just getting so tired of planning my whole life around making sure I don't run into those reminders. \n\nIt sounds a bit silly when I say it out loud, doesn't it? A grown man avoiding his own backyard and his family. I really want to get back to normal so I don't have to be such a bother to everyone with all my excuses. Does that make sense to you? I hope I'm doing the right thing by trying to finally tackle this.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the unease you feel is what's causing you to avoid the workshop and your brother-in-law?\n\nPatient: Yes, exactly, Doctor. Like I was just mentioning a moment ago, that... that feeling is the whole reason I keep steering clear of my own workshop and those family get-togethers where he might be. \n\nIt really is why I finally came in to see you. I'm just so tired of planning my whole life around making sure I don't run into those reminders. I hope I'm not repeating myself too much, I just want to make sure I'm answering you properly. I know it must sound a bit ridiculous for a man my age to act this way, but... yes, that unease is definitely what's causing it. Am I doing okay explaining all this to you? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need to know.\n\nDoctor: Did something specific and upsetting happen during the disagreement with your brother-in-law?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I suppose you could say it was upsetting, yes. But I really don't want to make it sound like some big, dramatic affair. We just... we had a disagreement, that's all. \n\nI mean, brothers-in-law argue sometimes, don't they? It's just a normal family thing, I'm sure. Voices got a little loud, and... well, things were said, but I'm sure he didn't mean anything by it. I don't want to paint him in a bad light, he's generally a good man. \n\nI just... I don't know, it just rattled me a bit more than it probably should have. I'm sorry, I know that's not a very specific answer. Am I being helpful at all? I just don't want to make a mountain out of a molehill, you know?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I wouldn't say it's ruining my relationships or anything awful like that. I still manage all the basics, you know, being a good husband and grandfather. I try to make sure everyone is taken care of. 

But... well, I suppose I have stopped doing a few things. I used to do some regular volunteer jobs that involved using the workshop, building little things for the community center, and I've had to back out of those. And the big family events, like I mentioned... I just try to find a polite excuse to skip them so I don't cause a scene. 

Even my painting, which I usually love... it just comes in fits and starts now, mostly because I kept all my good supplies out in the shop, and I just... I don't like going out there to get them. 

I really try not to let it bother anyone else, though. I hope I'm not sounding like a terrible burden on my family. Do you think they understand why I'm being like this? I just want to make sure I'm not doing too much damage while I try to get this sorted out.

I just want to make sure I'm not doing too much damage while I try to get this sorted out.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you feel like your reaction to the disagreement with your brother-in-law is disproportionate to what actually happened?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 76 / not_in_workforce / high_school\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I... I suppose you could say that, yes. I mean, looking back at it, it really wasn't anything out of the ordinary for two men to get a little heated over a disagreement. 

I know I shouldn't be letting it get to me this much. It's just a little squabble, really. I feel a bit foolish even bringing it up, to be honest. I'm a grown man, I should be able to shake off a few sharp words from my brother-in-law without avoiding my own workshop. 

I just... I don't want you to think I'm weak or overly sensitive, Doctor. I know it's silly to be so bothered by it. Do you think I'm making too big a deal out of this? I'm really trying to be sensible about it, I promise.

I don't feel like I'm actually right back there in the room with him or anything frightening like that. I always know I'm just standing at my bathroom sink, or sitting in my living room. \n\nI really hope I didn't make it sound worse than it actually is when we were talking earlier. I wouldn't want you to think I'm losing my grip on reality or making a mountain out of a molehill! It's really just a bothersome memory that passes by, and I know right where I am the whole time. I hope I'm explaining that alright? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: Are there other things or situations that you avoid besides the workshop and your brother-in-law?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, Doctor, just those two things, really. I try very hard not to let this whole silly business dictate my life, you know? It's just the workshop and... well, him. \n\nI really do make an effort to go everywhere else I normally would. I still go to the grocery store, and the pharmacy, and I take my walks around the neighborhood just like always. I wouldn't want you to think I'm hiding away in my house or anything foolish like that! I'm keeping up with my usual routine as best as I can, aside from that one little area. I hope that shows I'm trying to be sensible about all this... does that sound like I'm doing the right thing?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like this unease has significantly impacted your overall quality of life or relationships with people other than your brother-in-law?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no, I don't think so. I really try my best not to let it spill over into everything else. My relationships with the rest of the family and my friends are just fine, really. Like I mentioned before, I might skip a big gathering if I know he's going to be there, just to keep the peace, but I still call my kids every week and chat with the neighbors over the fence. I make absolutely sure of it. \n\nI really wouldn't say my whole quality of life is ruined or anything dramatic like that. I'm still enjoying a quiet retirement, for the most part. I just... well, I try to focus on the good things so I don't become a burden or a downer to anyone else. I hope that's the right way to look at it? I really am trying to handle it all as best as I can. Does it seem like I'm managing okay to you?\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid the workshop or your brother-in-law because you're trying to prevent yourself from feeling anxious or uneasy?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, Doctor, I suppose that's exactly it. I don't like to admit it, because it feels a bit silly for a man my age to act this way, but I really just want to keep from feeling that... that bother. \n\nThat's actually the main reason I finally decided to come in and talk to you. I just keep steering clear of my own workshop, and, well, any family get-together where my brother-in-law might show up. I've been telling myself I'm just keeping the peace, but after all this time, I'm honestly just tired of planning my whole life around not running into those reminders. \n\nI hope you don't think less of me for letting it get to me like this. I really don't want to sound weak or foolish. I just want to handle things the right way, and I figured it was time to ask for a little advice. Do you think it makes sense that I'd get tired of avoiding it all? I hope I did the right thing by coming in.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that happened in the past?\n\nPatient: Well... oh, I don't know if \"guilty\" is the right word. It's just... sometimes I get to thinking, and I can't help but feel like I... I should have handled things differently. \n\nI don't want to sound like I'm throwing a pity party, but I keep telling myself I should have just put my foot down sooner, you know? I never should have let him stay with us in the first place. I just... I think maybe none of this would have happened if I'd just handled it like a man right from the start. \n\nI know it's probably silly to dwell on it, and I really try not to let it get me down too much. I hope you don't think I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself. It's just a thought that creeps in sometimes. Is it normal to feel a little responsible like that? I'm trying to look at it the right way, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious most of the time, even when you're not thinking about the workshop or your brother-in-law?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no, Doctor, I wouldn't say that at all. Most of the time, I'm right as rain. I try to stay busy, you know, puttering around the yard or reading the paper. I really do try to keep a positive attitude about things. \n\nIt's really only when those specific things come up that I feel that... that tightness. Otherwise, I'm just living a quiet life, same as anyone else my age. I'm not walking around wringing my hands all day, I promise you that. I don't want you to think I'm completely falling apart or anything. Does that sound about right to you? I hope I'm explaining it clearly enough.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your overall daily functioning or relationships outside of avoiding the workshop and your brother-in-law?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I suppose there are a few little things, but nothing too terrible, I promise. I mean, I still manage all the basics just fine—getting dressed, making meals, all that. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around doing nothing. \n\nBut, well... I used to do some regular volunteer work, you know, fixing things up for the church bazaar and things like that. I've sort of... stepped back from that, just because it usually meant I needed to use the workshop, or it involved big family events where... well, where he might be. And my painting—I used to paint a fair bit—it's been coming in fits and starts lately. Most of my good supplies are out in the shop, and I just haven't felt up to going out there to get them. \n\nI know it probably sounds like I'm letting this beat me, and I really feel foolish even admitting it. I'm trying my best to keep up with things, I really am. Do you think it's awful that I've let it affect my hobbies like that? I hope you understand I'm not trying to be lazy or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still avoid the workshop and your brother-in-law if you didn't feel uneasy about it?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, Doctor, I don't think so. If I didn't get that... that uneasy feeling, I'd be out there tinkering every day, just like I used to. \n\nHonestly, that's really the main reason I finally decided to come in and talk to you. I just keep steering clear of my own workshop, and... well, any family get-together where my brother-in-law might show up. After all this time, I'm just getting so tired of planning my whole life around making sure I don't run into those reminders. \n\nIt sounds a bit silly when I say it out loud, doesn't it? A grown man avoiding his own backyard and his family. I really want to get back to normal so I don't have to be such a bother to everyone with all my excuses. Does that make sense to you? I hope I'm doing the right thing by trying to finally tackle this.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the unease you feel is what's causing you to avoid the workshop and your brother-in-law?\n\nPatient: Yes, exactly, Doctor. Like I was just mentioning a moment ago, that... that feeling is the whole reason I keep steering clear of my own workshop and those family get-togethers where he might be. \n\nIt really is why I finally came in to see you. I'm just so tired of planning my whole life around making sure I don't run into those reminders. I hope I'm not repeating myself too much, I just want to make sure I'm answering you properly. I know it must sound a bit ridiculous for a man my age to act this way, but... yes, that unease is definitely what's causing it. Am I doing okay explaining all this to you? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need to know.\n\nDoctor: Did something specific and upsetting happen during the disagreement with your brother-in-law?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I suppose you could say it was upsetting, yes. But I really don't want to make it sound like some big, dramatic affair. We just... we had a disagreement, that's all. \n\nI mean, brothers-in-law argue sometimes, don't they? It's just a normal family thing, I'm sure. Voices got a little loud, and... well, things were said, but I'm sure he didn't mean anything by it. I don't want to paint him in a bad light, he's generally a good man. \n\nI just... I don't know, it just rattled me a bit more than it probably should have. I'm sorry, I know that's not a very specific answer. Am I being helpful at all? I just don't want to make a mountain out of a molehill, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your avoidance of the workshop and your brother-in-law is significantly impacting your relationships with your family members other than your brother-in-law?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say it's ruining my relationships or anything awful like that. I still manage all the basics, you know, being a good husband and grandfather. I try to make sure everyone is taken care of. \n\nBut... well, I suppose I have stopped doing a few things. I used to do some regular volunteer jobs that involved using the workshop, building little things for the community center, and I've had to back out of those. And the big family events, like I mentioned... I just try to find a polite excuse to skip them so I don't cause a scene. \n\nEven my painting, which I usually love... it just comes in fits and starts now, mostly because I kept all my good supplies out in the shop, and I just... I don't like going out there to get them. \n\nI really try not to let it bother anyone else, though. I hope I'm not sounding like a terrible burden on my family. Do you think they understand why I'm being like this? I just want to make sure I'm not doing too much damage while I try to get this sorted out.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your reaction to the disagreement with your brother-in-law is disproportionate to what actually happened?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I suppose you could say that, yes. I mean, looking back at it, it really wasn't anything out of the ordinary for two men to get a little heated over a disagreement. \n\nI know I shouldn't be letting it get to me this much. It's just a little squabble, really. I feel a bit foolish even bringing it up, to be honest. I'm a grown man, I should be able to shake off a few sharp words from my brother-in-law without avoiding my own workshop. \n\nI just... I don't want you to think I'm weak or overly sensitive, Doctor. I know it's silly to be so bothered by it. Do you think I'm making too big a deal out of this? I'm really trying to be sensible about it, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Has this unease you've been feeling lasted for less than 6 months?\n\nPatient: Well, no, it's... it's actually been a bit longer than that. I didn't want to waste your time or anyone else's if it was just a passing phase. \n\nDoes that make it sound much worse, Doctor?
